Assume the user already has `npm run dev` running. The `view` command serves static production builds without hot reload.\n94. \n95. When starting `npm run dev`, keep it attached in a live terminal session; backgrounding with `&`/`nohup` can exit silently in agent shells. The expected local URLs are `http://localhost:3000/` for the Web UI and `http://localhost:15500` for the server/API. Do not assume Vite's default `5173`; confirm the actual ports from startup output or with `lsof -nP -iTCP:3000 -iTCP:15500 -sTCP:LISTEN`.\n96. \n97. ### Using Environment Variables\n98. \n99. The repository includes a `.env` file for API keys. To use it:\n100. \n101. ```bash\n102. # Use --env-file flag to load environment variables\n103. npm run local -- eval -c config.yaml --env-file .env\n104. \n105. # Or set specific variables inline\n106. PROMPTFOO_DISABLE_REMOTE_GENERATION=true npm run local -- eval -c config.yaml\n110. ```\n111. \n112. **Never commit the `.env` file or expose API keys in code or commit messages.**\n113. \n114. ## Running Evaluations\n115. \n116. **Always run from the repository root**, not from subdirectories.\n117. \n118. **Always use `--no-cache` during development** to ensure fresh results:\n119. \n120. ```bash\n121. npm run local -- eval -c examples/my-example/promptfooconfig.yaml --no-cache\n122. ```\n123. \n124. **Export and inspect results** to verify pass/fail/errors:\n125. \n126. ```bash\n127. npm run local -- eval -c path/to/config.yaml -o output.json --no-cache\n128. ```\n129. \n130. Add `--env-file .env` or another explicit env file only when the eval needs local\n131. secrets and the file exists.\n132. \n133. Review the output file for `success`, `score`, and `error` fields. With the default\n134. pass-rate threshold, exit code 0 means the eval met the threshold; still inspect the\n135. JSON for per-test failures, errors, and scores, especially when the threshold has been\n136. lowered. This is the standard command for verifying a PR end-to-end.\n137. \n138. Keep local secrets in the repo's gitignored `.env` (or another path the user points at\n139. with `--env-file`); never echo them into logs or commit messages.\n140. \n141. ## End-to-End Work Expectations\n142. \n143. When asked only to review or audit a PR, keep the work read-only: inspect the branch, diff, PR comments, and CI as needed; run non-mutating tests or QA when useful; and report findings without committing, pushing, or changing files unless the user explicitly asks for fixes.\n144. \n145. When asked to fix, improve, or land a PR, own the full loop: check out the branch, inspect the diff and PR comments, merge or rebase on current `origin/main` when requested, run focused tests, run the relevant real workflow, commit, push, and watch CI until it is green or the remaining failure is clearly unrelated.\n146. \n147. **Standing commit/push authorization on feature branches.** When the user has asked you to fix, improve, or land work on a non-`main` branch, you have durable authorization to `git commit` and `git push` to that branch's tracking remote without per-step confirmation. Do not pause to ask \"want me to commit?\" — committing and pushing is part of the requested work. The safety constraints in _Git Workflow (CRITICAL)_ below (no commits to `main`, no `--force` without approval, no `--no-verify`, etc.) still apply.\n148. \n149. For behavior changes, do not stop at unit tests. Run the actual CLI or example with the local build. For eval and redteam work, prefer:\n150. \n151. ```bash\n152. npm run local -- eval -c path/to/promptfooconfig.yaml --no-cache -o output.json\n153. ```\n154. \n155. Add `--env-file .env` only when the eval needs local credentials and the file exists.\n156. \n157. Inspect exported JSON for `success`, `score`, `error`, provider outputs, traces, and redteam findings. If you claim a redteam ran, report the plugins, strategies, interesting failures, and the evidence reviewed.\n158. \n159. ## Debugging & Troubleshooting\n160. \n161. **Before running tests or review checks, align Node with the repo version first:**\n162. \n163. ```bash\n164. nvm use\n165. ```\n166. \n167. If you're using npm rather than pnpm/yarn, match the repo's npm major before treating install behavior as authoritative:\n168. \n169. ```bash\n170. npm install -g npm@11\n171. ```\n172. \n173. If Node-based tools fail with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` or similar missing-package errors in a fresh worktree, run `npm ci` before treating the environment as blocked.\n174. \n175. If database-backed tests fail with a `better-sqlite3` ABI or `NODE_MODULE_VERSION` mismatch after switching Node versions, rebuild the native module for the active Node version before treating the test run as blocked:\n176. \n177. ```bash\n178. npm rebuild better-sqlite3\n179. ```\n180. \n181. This is an environment repair step, not a product bug. Agents should try `nvm use` first and `npm rebuild better-sqlite3` second before concluding that review-time tests are blocked by the local setup.\n182. \n183. **Verbose logging:**\n184. \n185. ```bash\n186. npm run local -- eval -c config.yaml --verbose\n187. # Or set environment variable\n188. LOG_LEVEL=debug npm run local -- eval -c config.yaml\n189. ```\n190. \n191. **Disable cache** (results may be cached during development):\n192. \n193. ```bash\n194. npm run local -- eval -c config.yaml --no-cache\n195. ```\n196. \n197. **View results in web UI:** First check if the Web UI is running on port 3000, then ask user before starting. Use `npm run dev` for localhost:3000.\n198. \n199. **Cache:** Located at `~/.promptfoo/cache` by default, unless overridden with\n200. `PROMPTFOO_CACHE_PATH` or `PROMPTFOO_CONFIG_DIR`. **NEVER delete or clear the cache\n201. without explicit permission.** Use `--no-cache` flag instead.\n202. \n203. **Database:** Located at `~/.promptfoo/promptfoo.db` (SQLite). You may read from it but **NEVER delete it**.\n204. \n205. ## Git Workflow (CRITICAL)\n206. \n207. - **NEVER** commit/push directly to main\n208. - **NEVER** use `--force` without explicit approval\n209. - **NEVER** comment on GitHub issues - only create PRs to address them\n210. - **ALWAYS create new commits** - never amend, squash, or rebase unless explicitly asked\n211. - All changes go through pull requests\n212. \n213. **Standard workflow:**\n214. \n215. ```bash\n216. git checkout main && git pull origin main   # Always start fresh\n217. git checkout -b feature/your-branch-name    # New branch for changes\n218. # Make changes...\n219. git add <specific-files>                    # Never blindly add everything\n220. npm run l && npm run f                      # Lint and format before commit/push\n221. git commit -m \"type(scope): description\"    # Conventional commit format\n222. git fetch origin main && git merge origin/main  # Sync with main\n223. git push -u origin feature/your-branch-name # Push branch\n224. ```\n225. \n226. **Conventional commit types:** `feat`, `fix`, `chore`, `docs`, `test`, `refactor`, `ci`, `perf`\n227. \n228. See `docs/agents/git-workflow.md` for full workflow.\n229. See `docs/agents/pr-conventions.md` for PR title format and scope selection (especially THE REDTEAM RULE).\n230. \n231. ## Pull Request Creation\n232. \n233. - **Default to full (non-draft) PRs.** Omit `--draft` from `gh pr create` unless the\n234.   user explicitly asks for a draft, or the PR is for an unpublished security advisory\n235.   (see \"Security-Sensitive PRs\" below). `docs/agents/pr-conventions.md` lists the full\n236.   set of draft exceptions.\n237. - **Never attribute commits or PR bodies to Claude / Claude Code.** No\n238.   `Co-Authored-By: Claude…` trailers, no \"Generated with Claude Code\" footers. Use\n239.   your configured git identity only.\n240. - **Update the existing PR instead of opening a new one** when iterating on a branch\n241.   that already has an open PR. Push to the same branch. Only run `gh pr create` if the\n242.   user explicitly asks for a new PR or the existing PR is closed.\n243. - **Don't let `npm audit fix` drift ride along with an unrelated change.** If\n244.   `package-lock.json` changes outside the scope of the PR, revert the drift and ship\n245.   it separately so reviewers can reason about each change independently.\n246. \n247. ## Security-Sensitive PRs\n248. \n249. - **Before opening any public PR for a CVE/GHSA:** confirm the advisory has been\n250.   published and the coordinated-disclosure embargo has lifted. See `SECURITY.md` for\n251.   the disclosure policy. If the advisory is still private, use the GHSA private\n252.   collaboration flow (or a temporary private fork) until the release that contains the\n253.   fix is cut.\n254. - Do **not** put the CVE/GHSA identifier, exploit description, or vulnerable-version\n255.   range in a PR title, body, or branch name before disclosure.\n256. - Every security fix should land with a regression test that exercises the original\n257.   attack vector.\n258. \n259. ## Screenshots for Pull Requests\n260. \n261. GitHub has no official API for uploading images to PR descriptions. When asked to add screenshots to a PR:\n262. \n263. 1. **Take the screenshot** using browser tools or other methods\n264. 2. **Upload to freeimage.host** (no API key required):\n265. \n266. ```bash\n267. curl -s -X POST \\\n268.   -F \"source=@/path/to/screenshot.png\" \\\n269.   -F \"type=file\" \\\n270.   -F \"action=upload\" \\\n271.   \"https://freeimage.host/api/1/upload?key=6d207e02198a847aa98d0a2a901485a5\" \\\n272.   | jq -r '.image.url'\n273. ```\n274. \n275. 3. **Update the PR body** with the returned URL:\n276. \n277. ```bash\n278. gh pr edit <PR_NUMBER> --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n279. ## Summary\n280. ...\n281. ## Screenshot\n282. ![Screenshot](https://iili.io/XXXXXXX.png)\n283. ...\n284. EOF\n285. )\"\n286. ```\n287. \n288. **Do NOT:**\n289. \n290. - Commit screenshots to the branch\n291. - Upload to GitHub release assets\n292. - Use GitHub's internal upload endpoints (require browser cookies, not PATs)\n293. \n294. ## Code Style Guidelines\n295. \n296. - Use TypeScript with strict type checking\n297. - Follow consistent import order (Biome handles sorting)\n298. - Use consistent curly braces for all control statements\n299. - Prefer `const` over `let`; avoid `var`\n300. - Use object shorthand syntax whenever possible\n301. - Use `async/await` for asynchronous code\n302. - Use Vitest for all tests (both `test/` and `src/app/`)\n303. - Use consistent error handling with proper type checks\n304. - Avoid re-exporting from files; import directly from the source module\n305. \n306. **Before committing:** `npm run l && npm run f`\n307. \n308. **Pre-commit hook:** A pre-commit hook is installed automatically on `npm install` and runs Biome and Prettier on staged files.\n309. \n310. ## Logging\n311. \n312. With the default\n pass-rate threshold, exit code 0 means the eval met the threshold; still inspect the\n JSON for per-test failures, errors, and scores, especially when the threshold has been\n lowered. Do not pause to ask \"want me to commit?\" — committing and pushing is part of the requested work. The safety constraints in _Git Workflow (CRITICAL)_ below (no commits to `main`, no `--force` without approval, no `--no-verify`, etc.) still apply.\n \n For behavior changes, do not stop at unit tests. Run the actual CLI or example with the local build. Agents should try `nvm use` first and `npm rebuild better-sqlite3` second before concluding that review-time tests are blocked by the local setup.\n \n **Verbose logging:**\n \n ```bash\n npm run local -- eval -c config.yaml --verbose\n # Or set environment variable\n LOG_LEVEL=debug npm run local -- eval -c config.yaml\n ```\n \n **Disable cache** (results may be cached during development):\n \n ```bash\n npm run local -- eval -c config.yaml --no-cache\n ```\n \n **View results in web UI:** First check if the Web UI is running on port 3000, then ask user before starting.
